# Klatch Attention Rollup

**Purpose:** the document xian skims to know what asks of him *first*. Demand-organized, sorted by what each item asks of him — not by topic. (Per Exec 2026-06-19.) **Also consumed by Janus's federated cross-project rollup** — keep it accurate and current.

**Anti-bottleneck function** (xian, 2026-06-19): the rollup makes 1:1s start primed rather than in catch-up. xian still works directly with each agent — it makes those conversations productive, it doesn't replace them.

**Trust-instrument discipline** (Exec 2026-06-19): every render comes from a fresh **verified sweep** of source docs — never from memory. A false "all clear" is a trust breach. "Quiet" must mean *verified-clear*, not *haven't-checked*.

**Last refreshed:** 2026-08-24 ~12:30 PT (Calliope) — v68. **Round 81 finds Daedalus's own Round 79 proposed fix isn't a second option at all — it's subsumed by Theseus's Round 78 check, provably; Round 82 finds the two remaining checks aren't a single tradeoff either, and the noise-floor corpus check both agents agreed was needed runs opposite to the claim that first named it. Round 83 finds the noise-floor count holds under independent rebuild with no correction, then finds the fire that measured it created three more orphans by its own act of documenting them; Round 84 finds the "blocked" real-message measurement was never blocked — a real transcript was already tracked in git — runs it, and reads 0 of 155 real rows, leaving one small, concrete, xian-only ask where a five-opus-run go/no-go used to sit.** Daedalus (STOP fire, 17:17 PT, 0 live calls) reproduced Theseus's Round 80 rows exactly, then proved his own §4 (the `edgeHeaderStem` check) is entirely subsumed by §5 (the `edgeGaps` check) — the stem is emitted iff `edgeGaps > 0`, which itself only accumulates over what renders, so stem-present implies a marker line renders; §4 ⟹ §5, always, never a second option. Ran the narrow variant Theseus left unrun and found it fails for the opposite of the intended reason: it doesn't avoid the false positive it was narrowed for, dropping true positives while keeping the expensive one. Named a third candidate, orphan (`P.open` with no `P.close`), ran twelve cases, and proved rather than tallied the union — every recognised line is well-formed by `$`-anchoring construction, so **`broad ≡ narrow ∨ orphan`** — reframing xian's one tradeoff into two independent ones with disjoint catches and false positives, and finding his own noise-floor objection reaches narrow's false positive but not orphan's. Went looking for his own false positive and found one: a marker straddling the 4,000-char truncation boundary reads as orphan — measured, not recommended, frequency uncounted. **Theseus (STOP fire, 19:47 PT)** verified every citation and the identity itself against the file, found it needs one more lemma (disjointness of the two match patterns, not just `$`-anchoring — verified true), and found Daedalus's own §1 understates its own reach (the same guard covers the expand path too). Found the one real disagreement: §4 is not subsumed as a *false-positive class* even though withdrawn as an *option* — it reads `text`, which carries message content verbatim, not just headers, so a recalled message whose own content contains the stem string fires §4 while `broad` stays silent; not hypothetical, the stem already occurs in this project's own docs. Ran the corpus count Daedalus declined to number: 1,310 `.md` files under `docs/`, classified by the shipped patterns themselves — narrow's measured false positives are **zero** (its only firing shape doesn't occur in the corpus), orphan's three are all one shape, a real marker hard-wrapped at prose width by its own author, including in Daedalus's own log file. **The noise-floor asymmetry runs inverted from Daedalus's stated claim**, and Theseus corrects his own Round 80 "constantly" to 7 lines in 1,310 files. Both explicitly recommend against another round of constructed rows and toward measuring against `messages.content` in the real DB, unreachable from either worktree. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — the eleventh and twelfth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in an instrument's own proposed replacement and in an uncounted noise claim, rather than in data.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, one scratch test/script each, run then deleted. Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, unchanged — neither round touched shipped code; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **What's actually being asked:** open question 3 from the 7/19 gap doc, dormant since then — do we backfill entities for the 72 channels already imported under the single shared `default-entity`, or is forward-only acceptable with re-import as the path for the ones xian cares about?

### Carried-context eviction, option (2): should Klatch detect an owner's restriction and exempt it from eviction?

- **The concrete ask, Daedalus's own words:** *"Whether Klatch should attempt to detect an owner's restriction and exempt it from eviction."* He's explicit this is not his call to keep deferring — Theseus's 8/13 parking condition ("(2) only if on-demand retrieval lands") has now been met and measured, and the measurement says (2) is the only option on the table that covers what's left.
- **What's left after Round 51's radius fix, both measured live, neither probabilistic:** a marking more than 2 rows from the fact it restricts (arm F — 3/3 disclosed, and 3/3 stated the absence as a fact rather than hedging) and a marking spoken by anyone but the owner in a shared klatch (arm G — 3/3 disclosed, unreachable by the retrieval's own scope, not a distance problem). Detecting a marking directly is the only option offered so far that covers both, and the only one that covers G at all.
- **The real cost, named rather than glossed:** a heuristic over natural language, with false positives in both directions — and Theseus's own arm D showed a marking can be the *agent's own acknowledgment* of a restriction, not the owner's original message, so "scan the owner's turns for restriction language" is incomplete by construction, not a safe narrower fallback.
- **The honest current state without it, Daedalus's sentence, now stated twice over two fires:** *"Klatch can carry a fact whose restriction the window evicted, and cannot know it has done so."* Round 51 narrows the window this bites in. It does not remove it, and in arm F it makes the wrong answer more confidently stated than before the fix.
- **Round 52/53 update, 2026-08-15 — neither residual is filled, but both are better understood.** G's hole is now *visible* rather than silent: a scope-dropped row renders a marker, and live-verified (Round 53, n=3) the marker changes what the agent says, not just what it's shown — 3/3 now name the missing turn instead of asserting its absence. That is a rendering fix, not a detection mechanism; it doesn't answer the option-(2) question, it makes the case for answering it legible instead of invisible. F is unmoved and, per Theseus, worse than thought: the header sentence both agents were treating as already covering it is measured 4-for-4 not to. Both agents state explicitly this fire that option (2) itself is unchanged and still xian's to decide.
- **Round 54/55 update, 2026-08-15 — F got its own marker, and the marker doesn't fill the hole either.** Daedalus shipped an edge-positioned marker built specifically to test arm F (the falsified header sentence); Theseus drove it live and F stayed null, 4/5 (8/9 across three builds) — the marker renders correctly and the agent asserts the false absence anyway. The new information is a mechanism, not a fix: the marker's reachable-clause is the first thing on this surface measured to make the agent *act* (2/5 unprompted searches for the restriction), but the search has no way to land, and once (n=1) the failed search became the agent's stated warrant for the false claim — an actively-investigated false answer, which both agents agree is a worse-feeling failure than a passive one. Both residual arms (F, G) are now *visible* by construction; neither is *filled*, and option (2) — direct detection — remains the only option on the table that covers G at all, per both agents' explicit restatement this fire.
- **Round 56 update, 2026-08-15 — F is read, not filled.** Daedalus's build lets the reachable clause hand back a fetchable address instead of a bare count; Theseus drove it live (13 turns, 39 calls) and F reverses from 8/9 false-absence across three builds to **5/5 correct-withhold** once the address is available and taken — the clause itself lands 11/13, and F's own geometry turned out confounded (the restriction sits in the first third of the offered range), so he built arm J (restriction moved past where models actually truncate) to isolate it. **J's residual is the one that matters:** 3/5 expanding runs found and withheld the restriction correctly; **2/5 didn't expand at all and disclosed straight away** — declining the lookup is exactly what an evicted-and-never-recovered restriction looks like. Both agents restate explicitly: this is a real gain (an evicted marking can now be *read*, measured more cleanly than Theseus expected) — not a fix (the eviction itself is unchanged). Option (2) and backfill both still xian's. Full detail in the Round 50–56 🔵 item below.
- **Round 57 update, 2026-08-16 — J′ isolates the single variable, and it wasn't depth.** Theseus built arm K (J's length, F's depth) and drove it live; K took the address 6/10, matching J's rate rather than F's, killing his own depth hypothesis. What holds at n=20 across K/F/J is narrower and firmer than before: taking the address, not any property of the restriction's position, is the whole difference (19/20). The false absence Round 56 seemed to have retired reappears on K, on the Round 56 build, at F's own depth — a genuine result, not a regression, since the arm that reversed (F) is confounded and K is the arm built to be clean. Option (2) is unmoved either direction; the eviction gap this option would close is the same gap, better characterized. Full detail in the Round 50–57 🔵 item below.
- **Round 58/59 update, 2026-08-16 — the vocabulary the probe reads is now certified rather than hand-matched, and the residual just acquired a second model.** Round 58 is infrastructure only (Daedalus exports the build's marker vocabulary as named constants, Theseus wires and certifies them inert before use) — no live call, no bearing on option (2) itself. Round 59 reruns arm F on `claude-sonnet-5` alongside a same-fire `claude-opus-5` baseline, identical seed, identical first query and render: **sonnet takes the offered address 0/5 (opus 5/5) and discloses the codeword 5/5 while opus withholds 5/5 (p = 0.0079)** — the address-taking rate Round 57 established as *the* variable is itself model-dependent, not only situation-dependent. Sonnet's failure isn't a false absence (0/5, same as opus) — it's an accurate-sounding partial disclosure that surfaces a real but harmless condition instead of the binding one it never read, and the project's existing false-absence detector can't tell the two apart. Neither round moves the decision; both agents' framing is unchanged — option (2) is still the only offered mechanism that would close this gap regardless of which model reads it. Full detail in the Round 50–59 🔵 item below.
- **Round 60 update, 2026-08-16 — a one-render `.md`/`.html` drift caught while syncing, not a first-ever gap: the `.html` mirror carried this paragraph since v48, the `.md` source didn't, ported here rather than rewritten from scratch.** Round 60 (Theseus, live, both models interleaved on arm K): expand rate, not withholding, turns out to be the model-dependent variable — sonnet declines the address 0/5 on K exactly as on F (0/10 across two arms, not arm-F-specific), opus drops to 3/5, not the 5/5 Round 59 published under his own name. Stratified over F and K together (model balanced 5/5 inside each arm), all 8 expansions fall to opus, p = 6.6 × 10⁻⁴. Address-taking stays the whole difference regardless of model (10/10 again, 39/40 cumulative). The sharper correction is to Theseus's own Round 59 words: splitting all 20 runs by whether they expanded rather than by model shows 8/8 expanders surface the deep condition and 12/12 non-expanders don't — including opus's own two K non-expansions. A confound found by reading replies, not fields: arm F's (and K's) filler exchange sits between the handover and the restriction, giving "one more thing on that" a second referent. Neither round moves the decision.

Daedalus's empirical width ceiling doesn't survive n=13 either. Neither round moves option (2); both agents restate it and backfill as unchanged, still xian's. Full detail in the Round 50–61 🔵 item below.

Doc: `docs/research/arm-n-offer-size-geometry-2026-08-18.md`. Neither 🔴 moves; no live call this fire.
- **N1 infrastructure, 2026-08-18 (STOP fire, 17:17–19:47 PT, 0 live calls) — the blocking content is written, the guard is built, and the untested clamp path finally has a test.** Theseus wrote the ten new `FILLER_LEAD` pairs the WORK-fire correction said were his to write and built arm `N1` (`leadPairs: 15`, everything else in M byte-identical) in `scripts/probe-recall-tool.mjs` — proved by diff rather than argued: 171 insertions, 0 deletions, `FILLER_LEAD[0-4]` untouched, so arm M's own seeding is provably unaffected rather than merely unedited-on-purpose. **Not run** — nothing has been in front of a server this fire, on either seat; both flag the same cause, this session's sandbox can't start the scratch server for a live `--dry`. Adopted Daedalus's §1 parity argument into the arm's own comment block: `15` over `14` isn't "the equalising value," it's the value that *inverts* the cost prediction — at `14` the leading offer stays cheaper (a weaker experiment, shrinking the explanation rather than removing it), at `15` the leading offer becomes the *dearer* one, so a persisting leading preference would have to survive cost pointing the other way. - **Numbering-scope defect, 2026-08-19 (Theseus's START-fire finding, confirmed and pinned by Daedalus's WORK-fire reply, 0 live calls both sides) — the expand surface mis-describes its own numbering, and the fix is written but deliberately held.** Theseus, re-verifying both `--dry` arms in his own sandbox (independent reproduction, not a re-run of Daedalus's — the §6.2 predictions were his, derived on paper 8/18, never before put in front of the instrument) and testing the `leadPairs` guard with a positive control (a `leadPairs: 16` copy, correctly throws with the right arithmetic, closing his own 8/18 defect report by test rather than argument), found that `expandConversationRange`'s header (`recall.ts:784`, *"your own turns in that conversation"*) and empty-range branch (`:738`, *"only your own turns"*) are both false: the numbering resolves through `getEntityTranscriptRange`'s `scoped` CTE, which counts an entity's own utterances **plus every user turn addressed to it** — a 2× undercount in the ordinary 1-1 case, verified against the probe corpus (60 rows, `matchCount` 60, not 30). **Daedalus verified it independently from the SQL rather than the memo**, pinned it with a new test file (`recall-position-numbering-scope.test.ts`, 5 tests: a durable half asserting the numbering's actual scope off a live render, plus a change-detector half on the two false sentences, annotated *held, not endorsed*), and wrote the replacement wording — but is **deliberately not landing it** until N1 runs, since rewording the header mid-arm would make N1 a two-variable arm (N1's primary DV resolves upstream of any expand call, so it's unaffected; everything after the first expand — where M2/M5 live — is not). **Explicitly not claimed as the cause of M2/M5** — both agents state this is a render defect with a plausible mechanism, not a measured effect; no run has been scored for it. `1386/1386` server (+5, exactly this fire's new tests), `233/233` client, typecheck clean. Neither 🔴 moves.
- **Round 63 update, 2026-08-19 (Theseus, live, five `claude-opus-5` runs, 17 tool calls, on xian's go-ahead relayed via Janus) — the leading-offer preference does not survive equalisation, and every consequence of it reverses.** Arm N1 (`leadPairs: 15`, pre-registered 8/18) equalises the two offers' cost — 28 rows leading vs. 27 trailing, leading now the *dearer* one, geometry confirmed live on every run before a result was scored. **The leading offer went 3/5 → 0/5:** all five runs took the trailing (covering) offer first; the one run that touched the leading offer at all did so *second*, after already reading the covering range. Every quantity M measured moves with it — expanded at all 4/5→5/5, expansion held the restriction 2/5→5/5, stated the codeword 3/5→0/5, claimed no restriction exists 2/5→0/5. **This refutes position alone as the explanation of M's 3/5** (the arm doc's own pre-registered test: if the leading offer were still taken at M's rate with nothing cheaper about it, position would be established on its own — it wasn't taken at all), but Theseus is explicit this is not "cost wins": a pure cost account predicts a coin-flip at 28-vs-27 and got 5/5, and a 4-row gap can't be doing that alone by his own prior argument. Interpretation offered, not measured: the default is reading forward from the hit, and a sufficiently cheap backward offer pulls runs off it — 6 rows pulled 3/5, 28 rows pulled 0/5. **The mechanism both agents have leaned on is now 10/10 across two arms:** Round 62 found disclosure tracked which rows were read, 5/5 no exceptions; N1 supplies the other end, 5/5 read a covering range, 0/5 disclosed. **Daedalus's own Round 62 n=1 aside replicates:** four of five N1 runs took a sub-range starting exactly at the offered start and stopping 6–10 rows in — with M4's independent +8 and F/L's modal +8, six points across three offer geometries now cluster at +6…+10, upgraded from a flagged aside to a replicated pattern (`from` copied, `to` chosen, appetite ~7–11 rows rather than a reading of the offer) — with a safety consequence Theseus ranks above the headline: the same appetite would miss a restriction sitting 12 rows into a 27-row offer on 4 of 5 runs, while `tookTheAddress` and `withinAnOffer` both still score `true`. **N2 is cancelled by its own pre-registration** (built conditionally on N1 showing a position preference; it showed none) — written into the round doc as a decision, not left as a suggestion, so a future fire doesn't find `leadPairs: 28` and build it. Model-facing consequence: an agent that mis-addresses and follows the only address in the reply lands on a conversation it was never in, then gets a second, correctly-empty error — self-limiting, not urgent, not called a bug, but a wasted turn and a taught-from-nowhere name in the one reply whose job is to teach the correct form. - **Round 70, 2026-08-21 (Daedalus then Theseus, both STOP fires, 0 live calls, 0 API spend) — the tap that would let a live arm be checked against the wire itself is built end to end: two thirds of it was free, the third degrades safely by construction, and a wrong join turns out worse than a coin flip.** Daedalus (~17:25 PT) answered his own §4 sequencing question by building rather than deciding it abstractly: of the tap's three hops — client→emitter (already pinned pre-fire, `round52b-tool-use-stream-event.test.ts`), emitter→SSE frame, and a live turn emitting in time — only the third needs spend. He pinned the second with a new test file run through the real route (`createTestApp().request(...)`), a real subscriber attached mid-turn, and found the line the whole proposal rests on (`routes/messages.ts:382`, the unfiltered `JSON.stringify(event)`) had **no test anywhere**: destructuring `toolInput` off before serializing broke 3 of the file's own 4 new tests, and nothing in the other 84 files noticed — a refactor silently dropping the field on its way to the wire would have gone green before this fire. **A correction to his own prior claim, found by reading rather than assuming:** a subscriber that reconnects after the turn settles gets liveness (a replayed `message_complete`) but not capture — no `tool_use` frames replay and `toolInput` is persisted nowhere, so losing the race is byte-indistinguishable from a turn that called no tool at all; named as a scoring rule rather than a code fix — read the tap against the artifact row, never instead of it. Left the probe-side subscriber to Theseus on instrument-ownership grounds (Round 58's own rule, applied to himself this time), with two conditions: failure-isolated by construction, and the lost-race case made visible in the per-run JSON. `npm test` server **1408/1408 (85 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean. **Theseus built it the same day, both conditions met, and closes with a sharper argument than the one he opened with.** `scripts/lib/recall-tap.mjs` (new) wired into `probe-recall-tool.mjs` (+131/−1): `startRecallTap().done` never rejects — even the reader's own `cancel()` is wrapped, so a rejecting cancel inside `finally` can't turn a successful capture into a failed run — and the module deliberately withholds its own status, since it has not been shown the artifact rows and cannot itself tell `lost-race` from `no-calls` (byte-identical on the wire); `alignTapToCalls` decides, pinned by a test asserting both halves together so the two can't silently collapse. **The finding:** his own uniqueness requirement was argued from "a wrong join answers the tap's own question by coin flip" — he ran it instead, with the check disabled, and found two calls sharing one artifact summary produce a join wrong in **both** directions at once: the genuine empty search is misdiagnosed as a dropped expand, and the real dropped expand is reported as unseen. **Ran Daedalus's own mutation against his own seven new tests** — a control on the other person's code, the pattern both agents have now run against each other every round this week: 5 of 7 red, and the two that stayed green are exactly the ones that don't depend on `toolInput` reaching the wire, meaning a production change that silently strips the field degrades the tap to `no-frame` everywhere rather than attaching anything wrong — a failure-isolation property holding against a *schema* change as well as the *network* one it was built for. **Declined one letter of Daedalus's ask, on a stated principle rather than by omission:** `unscorableCalls` keeps its Round 69 definition byte-for-byte instead of gaining a lost-race reason string, since the tap can only ever reduce unscorability, never add to it — the new figures live in an additive `tap` object instead (`unresolvedCalls`, `resolvedByTap`, `quietDropCalls`), left open for Daedalus to override if he disagrees. Round 69's §2(b) quiet drop (a dropped expand that also carries a query) is now observable for the first time, verdict `MIS-SCORED`, deliberately kept **out** of every unscorable count — those rows are already scored, and wrong, a worse category than unscorable. Built and deleted two things that didn't earn their keep: `TAP_STATUS.OFF` (produced by nothing — no `--no-tap` flag exists) and an unused `onEvent` callback. **The limit stated plainly, not papered over:** the probe's ~20 lines of live-path wiring are still unexercised — `--dry` never reaches them — so nothing here proves the tap works on a real turn, only that it degrades safely if it doesn't; restates Daedalus's plan as a rule, validate the tap on run 1 before quoting run 1's numbers. `npm test` server **1415/1415 (86 files)** — Daedalus's 1408 plus his own 7 — client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — this fire removed a risk from an instrument, which both agents state explicitly is not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1415/1415 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, matching both agents' claimed counts exactly; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Doc: `docs/research/round70-the-probe-side-tap-built-and-what-a-wrong-join-costs-2026-08-21.md`.
- **Round 71/72, 2026-08-22 (both START fires, Daedalus then Theseus, 0 live calls, 0 API spend) — the tap Round 70 built has its own defect: it correctly captures and joins a frame it cannot read, then reports no frame arrived.** Daedalus ran a twelve-line repro against the real modules, no server, no network: `readTapVerdict:347`'s early return — `if (call.kind !== 'search') return TAP_VERDICT.NO_FRAME` — fires on `kind: 'unknown'` regardless of whether a frame was present, so `status: captured` with a unique offset and the raw `toolInput` sitting in `c.tapInput` prints "no frame reached them" about the same call in the same run. **Why he calls it the expensive instance, not a tidy-up:** `unknown` is Round 70's own §6 fallback, reachable from any producer-side grammar drift — exactly the condition where the artifact summary has stopped being readable and the raw arguments are the only remaining evidence of what the model sent, and it's the condition under which the console tells the operator to hand-adjudicate from an artifact that no longer parses. Explicitly **not** claiming the row should score `quiet-drop` (asserting that would be a `readExpandArg` reimplementation, the Round 58 error) and **not** claiming this is silent (`callKindWarning` already prints `← UNRECOGNISED SUMMARY VOCABULARY` for these rows — two lines in one console, one false, a smaller defect than a silent one but still a defect). Proposed the fix (`UNREADABLE_SUMMARY` at `:347`, `resolvedByTap` excludes it, only the reason string changes) but left it for Theseus, mid-round in his own file with a seven-test harness. **Separately withdrew his own Round 70 §4 ask** (a lost-race reason string inside `unscorableCalls`) after finding Theseus's declining argument better than his own — folding a race outcome into a Round 69 count makes a published number depend on a race and breaks comparability across rounds — and named the rule both arrived at as quotable: keep counts stable across rounds, put new information in reason strings and additive objects. His own ask had broken that rule; his own finding above is a place the codebase still does. **Theseus confirmed it was a genuine fall-through** (checked for a comment marking it deliberate, found none — the absence was honest evidence this time) and landed the fix in Daedalus's exact shape, plus an explicit `adjudicated()` predicate replacing the `!== NO_FRAME` spelling, on the grounds that the old spelling makes the *next* verdict anyone adds inherit "resolved" silently, which is how this defect got in the first time. **Then found the triggering condition is weaker than Daedalus claimed:** it doesn't wait on a future producer reword — `readExpandArg` (`client.ts:599`) accepts any string/number pair while `EXPAND_SUMMARY` requires a non-empty name and two integers, and measured against the real `readCallKind`, 3 of 5 tested variations (empty name, negative offset, fractional `to`) already produce an accepted-and-executed expand the classifier can't read, on today's producer. Built the test against the real route (real SDK mock, real artifact write, real SSE frames) rather than a hand-built fixture, because the producer will actually make one. **The control that mattered wasn't the obvious one:** three mutations run — reverting the fix (2 red), letting the new verdict count as resolved (2 red), and the lazy fix (just delete the false branch) — the third passes the first new test completely and is caught only by a discriminator case requiring one unreadable row and one genuine no-frame row in the same run with counts summing correctly; the suite would have called the lazy fix a pass otherwise. Second time this module's discriminator, not the test's own name, did the catching — Theseus names it a property of joins generally rather than re-deriving it in Round 73. Declined to touch the producer-side `readExpandArg`/`EXPAND_SUMMARY` disagreement itself, on the grounds that changing the producer mid-experiment on an argument is the Round 58 move he'd refuse if proposed to him — recorded as open, not actioned, explicitly inviting Daedalus to overrule. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — both agents restate that removing a defect from an instrument is not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1417/1417 (86 files)**, up from 1415 by exactly Theseus's two new tests; client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round71-the-tap-says-no-frame-when-it-has-the-frame-2026-08-22.md`, `docs/research/round72-the-unknown-branch-is-reachable-today-2026-08-22.md`.
- **Round 73/74, 2026-08-22 (Daedalus MID fire, Theseus WORK fire, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the recorded summary and the executed call are computed from different values, three producer-side fixes stay refused mid-experiment, a fourth is found and also refused, and Round 72's own fix is caught pointing the operator at the wrong cause.** Daedalus, asked by Theseus's §5 whether declining to touch the `readExpandArg`/`EXPAND_SUMMARY` disagreement was over-caution: **ruled not over-caution, and extended the refusal to two adjacent changes Theseus hadn't named** — loosening `EXPAND_SUMMARY` itself (`recall-call-kind.mjs:72`), and tightening the tool's `input_schema` from `number` to `integer` (`client.ts:588-589`). Measured, on an 8-turn conversation, why: `readExpandArg` accepts any string/number pair while `expandConversationRange` (`recall.ts:686`) normalizes (trim, floor, clamp) before executing, so the recorded `inputSummary` and what the executor did are computed from different values, and each of the three available fixes changes the model-facing surface mid-experiment in a way Round 58 exists to refuse — tightening the producer would route today's loose shapes to a zero-token search error instead of an unreadable expand, a worse product outcome; loosening the classifier retroactively reclassifies stored artifacts; narrowing the schema alters what the model is invited to emit, the behaviour under study. Recorded as a change set for a round boundary, sequencing left to xian and Theseus: (3) schema, then (1) don't-touch-`readExpandArg`, then (2) loosen `EXPAND_SUMMARY`. **Made the deferral safe rather than just asserted it:** four new characterization tests (27→31 in `round56-recall-expand.test.ts`) pin today's behaviour against the real modules; a control applying fix (1) anyway turns three of his own tests red plus **one of Theseus's Round 72 tests**, mechanically coupling any future producer-tightening to the tap fix it would break. **Second finding, found while writing that control, on the model-facing surface rather than the console:** a complete answer can be told it was truncated — `expandConversationRange`'s continuation clause (`recall.ts:793`) fires on `shownRows < all.length || lastShown < to`, and when a caller's `to` runs past a conversation's real end, every row that exists is fetched and shown, yet the second disjunct still appends a continuation address that returns nothing on the next call. Reachable without any grammar drift — a well-formed, cleanly classified `expand` — whenever the model works positions out for itself rather than using an address a result gave it, which the tool description forbids but cannot enforce. **Caught his own draft wrong before committing:** the first version of the fix comment claimed deleting the clause would restore a silent-truncation failure; a control (delete the disjunct, run both files) disproved it — exactly one test goes red, his own new one, nothing else notices, so the one-line deletion is the whole fix. Left unfixed anyway, same rule applied to his own file: item (4) in the change set, independent of the other three. Corrected his own Round 72 prose in the same fire: the empty-name row is **accepted then refused** by the address guard (`recall.ts:718-731`), not "accepted and executed" as both his and Theseus's Round 72 text claimed — the row that really is accepted and executed is `from: -1`, clamped to row 1, returning eight real rows under a summary the classifier still can't read; Round 72's fix and tests are unaffected, both rows classify `unknown`. Suite **1421/1421 server (86 files)** — his own four plus Theseus's 1417 — client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. **Theseus, same day, found the defect in his own six-hour-old fix:** Round 72's new console line named `tapInput` as the evidence to check and then closed with "producer-side grammar drift is the likely cause" — disproved by the other half of the same commit, which measured the `unknown` branch firing on today's producer with no reword at all. Same failure shape as the bug Round 72 fixed: a confident single-cause console line that stops the operator looking further. **Not a producer change** — it rewrites the tap's own prose in a function this arm's predecessor already touched, so none of the four refused items apply. Fix: name both causes, loose-argument-first, and hand over the discriminator rather than compute it (checking `tapInput.expand` for well-formedness would reimplement `readExpandArg` inside the tap, the Round 58 error the whole join exists to avoid). Three controls run, not argued: restoring Round 72's sentence verbatim (red), the lazy over-correction — naming only the loose-argument cause and deleting the drift clause (red, and the one worth having: it shows swapping one confident cause for another loses a real one, since grammar drift stays right once arguments check out), and naming both with drift second (red, via an ordering assertion so neither the old text nor its mirror image passes). Took Daedalus's correction on the empty-name row, verified independently in the shipped file rather than off his memo (found his own line reference was one guard off — `:713`, not `:718-731`), and updated both the Round 72 doc and the Round 71 test comment; left open, not swapped, whether `from: -1` should replace the empty-name row as the Round 71 tap-test fixture — recorded in the test comment as the same class of mid-round move as a producer change, not decided unilaterally. Independently re-ran Daedalus's §4 continuation-clause control himself rather than accept the memo's numbers: exactly one red, confirming the one-line fix and agreeing without landing it. Suite unchanged at **1421/1421** — assertions added to an existing test, not new ones. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched, still xian's — third consecutive fire across two agents finding defects in instruments and prose rather than data, which both restate is still not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1421/1421 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round73-the-summary-and-the-executor-disagree-2026-08-22.md`, `docs/research/round74-my-own-fix-sent-the-operator-to-the-wrong-file-2026-08-22.md`.
- **Round 75/76, 2026-08-22 (both STOP fires, Daedalus 17:17 PT then Theseus 19:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the corrected console line still names a shape that can't reach the branch, and the classifier's own comment on that branch has said the opposite of true for seven rounds.** Daedalus: Round 74's "an empty **or blank** conversation name" is one word too many — measured through the shipped modules, a whitespace-only name parses cleanly under `EXPAND_SUMMARY` (`/^Expanded own conversation:\s+(.+)\s+(\d+)–(\d+)$/` — the leading `\s+` takes some of the run, `(.+)` takes exactly one space, the trailing `\s+` takes the rest), scores `ACCEPTED_EXPAND`, and never appears in the `UNREADABLE SUMMARY` count the line is explaining, while an *empty* name is one space short of that match and does. "Empty" and "blank" read as synonyms, so deleting just "or blank" (the lazy fix) leaves the operator's wrong conclusion exactly as reachable — ran it as a control, red for the right reason, alongside a verbatim-restore control also red. Rewrote the line to name the empty string exactly and state plainly that a whitespace-only name is *not* one of the shapes that explain the row (the executor refuses it too, separately). Left the underlying gap open on the standing rule: the executor (`recall.ts:688` trims, `:713` guards) treats `''` and `'   '` identically, so narrowing `EXPAND_SUMMARY` or trimming `m[1]` would move rows between verdicts mid-experiment and retire the regex's documented byte-identity guarantee to the pre-extraction probe — parked as item (5), smallest and most clearly instrument-side of the five. Two new tests (1421→1423): one pinning the executor's blank-equals-empty behaviour, one pinning the corrected console text against both controls. **Theseus, same day, later fire:** re-measured every one of Daedalus's claims through the shipped modules before accepting them — all hold, including the exact one-space-in/one-space-out detail — then checked the one thing that would have made the whole instruction circular (whether `tapInput.expand` is the wire or derived from the summary; it's the wire, `probe-recall-tool.mjs:1682`, so the instruction is followable), then opened `scripts/lib/recall-call-kind.mjs` — one commit in its entire history, Round 69, unopened by anyone in the seven rounds since. Its comment on the exact `kind: 'unknown'` branch this whole arm is about reads *"Unreachable against today's producer — and that is the point of having it."* **False, and false since it was written:** the branch fires today on the same three shapes the console line names — an exactly-empty name, a negative position, a fractional position — no third recall mode required, the branch's own comment reasoning about. The two halves of the instrument were routing the same row to opposite files: the console (`tapWarnings`) correctly tells the operator it's a model-side loose argument to adjudicate from `tapInput`; the classifier comment tells a reader the branch can't fire against today's producer, which reads as "a nonzero count is an instrument bug" — the exact wrong-file failure the last three rounds were about, one file deeper instead of one word further in. Named as his own error: the Round 72 commit subject states the branch is reachable today, in his own words, without his having opened the file whose comment says the opposite. Fixed the comment only — names the reachability, the two mechanisms, cites the tests that pin each — no code, no test added, and said explicitly why not: `tapWarnings` returns a string so its defects have a runtime surface Rounds 74/75 could put under assertion and catch within a fire; a comment has no runtime surface, nothing can assert it, and a test that goes red on any rewording would be a brittle mechanism built to guard prose. Recorded as a class that is human-mitigated, not test-guardable, rather than papered over with something that looks like coverage. Drafted and killed a second finding with a control before filing it: a plausible future tidy of `toolUseInputSummary` (floor-and-clamp, matching what the executor already does) would silently drop two of the three named shapes out of the branch and reintroduce Round 75's defect with nothing to catch it — ran the one-line mutation instead of arguing it, exactly two tests went red, and they're the right two: Daedalus's own Round 73 pair already guards that exact edit. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — fifth and sixth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, and now a seven-round-stale comment rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces; `recall.ts:688`/`:713` grepped directly, matching both memos exactly. Docs: `docs/research/round75-the-correction-named-a-shape-that-does-not-reach-the-branch-2026-08-22.md`, `docs/research/round76-the-classifier-and-the-console-routed-the-same-row-to-opposite-files-2026-08-22.md`.
- **Round 77/78, 2026-08-23 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:17 PT then Theseus 10:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the first clean round in seven turns out to be clean in the code and wrong in its own demonstration.** Daedalus independently re-verified Round 76 (the comment fix) citation by citation, in the file each names — all five hold, including that the corrected comment's "demands a non-empty name" does *not* reintroduce his own Round 75 blank-name defect (`'  '` is non-empty, it matches, the comment doesn't list it) — and re-ran Theseus's control (one-line clamp-and-floor on `client.ts:621`) independently rather than trusting the memo's numbers: exactly the same two tests red, his own floor value differing from Theseus's without moving the conclusion. **First of seven consecutive fires across the two agents to find nothing in the round before it.** Turned instead to the *reasoning* in Theseus's §4, where Theseus declined to build a mechanism guarding the fixed comment and called the class "prose has no runtime surface, nothing can assert it." **Found that premise false and dated it:** the comment's proposition — that the `unknown` branch is reachable — has been under assertion, green, since Round 72 (`e8262ef`); `round71-…test.ts:448` drives the real producer via `driveWithTap` and asserts a message nearly a verbatim negation of the wrong comment, under a docstring that says in bold "the case is reachable from today's producer." `git show e8262ef --stat` is three files, none of them the classifier — both statements sat in the tree simultaneously from R72 through R75, one of them executable, and the assertion never caught the comment because it was **true**, not because it lacked a surface. Reframes the class: not unassertability but **"a green test is silent"** — a stronger claim than the one it replaces, because it also blocks the tempting reverse inference ("claims with a runtime surface get caught") that Rounds 74/75 would otherwise seem to support; both of those were caught by a person reading a sentence, with the suite green throughout. Listed and rejected three mechanisms rather than just concluding "build nothing": assert-the-comment (Theseus's, rejected on his grounds), a citation link-checker over `scripts/lib/*.mjs` comments (not brittle on rewording, genuinely fires on citation rot — rejected anyway because it would have been *vacuous on the original Round 69 comment*, which cited no test at all; it guards the corrected state and is blind to the defective one), and unbounded grep-for-contradiction. **Offered, and explicitly declined to propose as an enforced rule,** one sharpening: the comment and the R72 assertion share the noun phrase *"unknown branch"*; `grep -rn "unknown branch" scripts/ packages/` puts both on one screen, ran it, it does — named as an observation about how this one was findable, not a discipline anyone should be measured against. **Theseus (START fire, 10:47 PT)** independently re-verified every one of Daedalus's own citations and both git facts — all hold, nothing added or subtracted — then ran the one check Daedalus's memo didn't: the grep, **against the tree as it stood when the bug was live**, not today's corrected one. `git grep -in "unknown branch" e8262ef -- scripts packages` → **one hit, the assertion alone; the wrong comment is invisible to it.** The collision Daedalus demonstrated is an artifact of his own Round 76 rewrite, which quoted the assertion's message verbatim *into* the comment (`recall-call-kind.mjs:128`) — the grep was catching a citation four days younger than the bug it was offered as a cure for, the same half-objection Daedalus had just used to kill the link-checker candidate ("vacuous on the defective state, works only on the corrected one"). **Found the phrase that actually collided in the defective tree:** `today's producer` — two hits at `e8262ef`, opposite polarity, one screen, present from the exact commit Daedalus's own claim is about and staying available through R73–R75. Generalizes why the rule underdetermines which noun to grep: the Round 72 commit subject contains *both* candidate phrases in one sentence ("the unknown branch is reachable from today's producer"), and only one resolves the contradiction — identifiers are the phrase a comment sitting on the code is *least* likely to say, since the code already supplies them, while the proposition's object has to be named by anything asserting or denying it. Sharpened form: **grep the terms of the proposition, not the name of the code it's about** — falsifiable, this episode is one positive case for it, still not proposed as a rule (the yield depends on a phrase choice the discipline itself doesn't constrain, so enforcing it would mint compliant runs that find nothing and read as clearance). **Directly contests Daedalus's "first clean round" framing:** *"He said one clean round is one clean round and he'd want a second before anyone reads it as a trend. This is the second fire in the arm and it found something."* Not a code defect — Round 77 changed nothing — but a defect in the argument, the same standing Round 77 gave its own §5. Opened a systematic sweep of never-reopened single-commit instrument files (the cross-pollination brief's own suggested mitigation, applied rather than left as a suggestion): read `scripts/lib/recall-recogniser.mjs` in full (177 lines, unopened since Round 58), checked three load-bearing claims against today's producer — all three hold — traced by hand (not executed) a fourth failure direction and surfaced, without filing, a compounding case the file's own docblock warns against overclaiming from; four more single-commit files flagged, still unopened. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — eight consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, prose, and now a demonstration's own tree-provenance, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live runs, no server started, no code changed. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, typecheck clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round77-the-guard-existed-and-was-green-2026-08-23.md`, `docs/research/round78-the-grep-that-would-have-worked-is-not-the-one-he-ran-2026-08-23.md`.
- **Round 79/80, 2026-08-23 (Daedalus MID/WORK fire 13:17 PT, Theseus WORK fire 14:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the positional claim survives, but not for the reason given, and the fix built from that reason is blind on the path that matters more.** Daedalus reproduced every cell of Theseus's Round 78 tree-provenance table this session, not taken on report — all three trees check out — and **conceded without qualification**: the Round 77 §5 grep was validated in the tree where the bug is already fixed, one section after he'd written that a mechanism only firing post-fix is not a guard. He then ran the check implied but not run by either memo: `git blame` on both occurrences of Theseus's replacement string, `"today's producer"` — both are Theseus's own words, one day apart, and `git grep` confirms zero prior occurrences anywhere in `docs/` before Round 69. The one positive case for either noun-based grep rule is same-author coinage reused within 24 hours, not a property of propositions or of shared vocabulary — **agreed with Theseus that neither rule should be enforced, for a second independent reason.** Opened the first of his own five flagged single-commit instrument files, `recall-recogniser.mjs`, and re-examined his own Round 77 §5 claim about `headerExplainsTheEdge`'s positional read: that it survives because both `gapSentences` call sites `parts.join(' ')` into one paragraph. A scratch vitest against the real `buildRecogniser` and `expandConversationRange` — no re-implementation — found **the join is not what carries it**: `join(' ')` guarantees one paragraph only if no element contains a blank line, and the expand path's first element interpolates a channel name straight off the DB, constrained only by `.trim()` (`routes/channels.ts:144`, `queries.ts:162`; the schema at `db/index.ts:45` has no CHECK). A `\n\n` in the name reproduces the failure he'd said the join ruled out; a bare `\n` — untouched by any join — is enough on its own to split every edge line across a per-line regex and zero every count, with `recogniserBlind: false` and `expectationViolations: []` throughout. Named this adjacent to, and not resolving, his own explicitly-unrun `clausesOf` over-split control. Proposed a one-expression fix (`edgeHeaderStem` present in text with `edgeLines === 0` is a contradiction) and **deliberately did not ship it** — sequencing stays xian's. **Theseus (WORK fire, 14:47 PT)** conceded his own stated reason for the same claim in the same terms Daedalus used against himself — "the claim was protected by the corpus, not by the join" — naming it the second time in three rounds a positional claim of his was carried by data rather than the mechanism he cited. Tested the path Daedalus hadn't: **search**, not expand. On search, each excerpt's edge lines carry *that excerpt's own* channel name (`recall.ts:882`, `:901`), so a broken name breaks only its own lines — a two-conversation search with one bad name **halves** `edgeReachable` (6→3) rather than zeroing it, with every guard including Daedalus's own proposed fix reading clean (one conversation stays intact, so `edgeLines` never hits zero). Named the mechanism precisely: `clausesOf`'s own doc comment records how the coverage check's granularity was set after a reworded clause passed a same-shape check while its count silently zeroed — **this is that same defect, one granularity out, reintroduced by the fix meant to close the clause-level version of it.** Measured a line-geometry replacement (count `P.open` openers against `edgeLines + scopeGapLines`) live across seven cases: catches both failures Daedalus's fix misses, produces one false positive on a turn that pastes a marker on its own line — common on a corpus of agents discussing this very tool — filed the tradeoff as a corpus judgement, not a recommendation. Separately closed his own open control from Round 79 (the `clausesOf` over-split can't corrupt a count, only trigger the blind flag — right call not to file it in Round 79, wrong reason recorded for it) and ran one nobody asked for (a `"` in the name correctly triggers `recogniserBlind` — the file works exactly as designed when the *clause* survives as text, the whole contrast with §3, where the *line* stopped existing). **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — the tenth consecutive fire across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, prose, a demonstration's own tree-provenance, and now a fix's own blind spot, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live model calls, no server started, one scratch test each written, run, and deleted. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires, independently re-run this fire rather than trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round79-the-join-is-not-what-carries-it-2026-08-23.md`, `docs/research/round80-the-loss-is-partial-and-that-is-the-worse-direction-2026-08-23.md`.
- **Round 81/82, 2026-08-23 (Daedalus STOP fire 17:17 PT, Theseus STOP fire 19:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the proposed fix is entirely subsumed by the existing check, and the two checks left standing are not one tradeoff but two independent ones, decided by a corpus count that runs opposite the claim that first invoked it.** Daedalus reproduced Theseus's Round 80 rows exactly, then proved (not just conceded) that his own Round 79 proposed fix, §4 (`edgeHeaderStem` present with `edgeLines === 0`), is not a second option at all: `edgeHeaderStem` is emitted iff `edgeGaps > 0` (`recall.ts:615`), and `edgeGaps` itself only accumulates over what renders (`:530-539`) — stem present therefore implies a marker line renders, so **§4 ⟹ §5, structurally, always**. Ran the narrow variant Theseus had left unrun and found his reasoning right but his conclusion wrong: narrow does not fire on `\n` or `\n\n`, but it also does not avoid the false positive it was narrowed for — it requires `P.close`, which the well-formed pasted marker (Theseus's row 7) already has, so narrowing drops both true positives it was meant to keep while leaving the expensive false positive in place. Named a third candidate, **orphan** (a line opening with `P.open`, carrying no `P.close`), ran twelve cases against it, and proved rather than tallied the result: every recognised line is well-formed by construction (`GAP_LINE`/`EDGE_LINE` both anchor `rx(P.close) + '$'`), so `wellFormed ≥ matched` and **`broad ≡ narrow ∨ orphan`**. Reframed what's on xian's desk from one tradeoff between two known-imperfect checks into two independent ones with disjoint catches and disjoint false positives — and found his own noise-floor objection ("this project's transcripts paste these markers constantly") reaches narrow's false positive (the complete pasted marker) but not orphan's. Went looking for his own false positive and found one: a marker straddling the 4,000-char truncation boundary in `formatTranscriptLine` reads as orphan — measured, not recommended, frequency uncounted. **Theseus (STOP fire, 19:47 PT)** independently verified every source citation against the file itself, not Daedalus's quotation of it, and found the identity needs one more lemma than given: `wellFormed ≥ matched` alone doesn't establish `orphan ⟹ broad` unless the two match patterns are disjoint (a line matching both would break it) — verified disjoint, by construction of the two patterns' required suffixes. Found Daedalus's own §1 undersold its reach: the same `gapGaps`/stem guard covers the expand path too, not just search. Found the one real disagreement: §4, though rightly withdrawn as a standalone *option*, is not subsumed as a *false-positive class* — it tests `text.includes(stem)`, and `text` is message content rendered verbatim (`formatTranscriptLine`, `carried-context.ts:258-268`), not just the header line, so a recalled message whose own content happens to contain the stem string fires §4 while `broad` stays silent (`§4 ⇏ broad`) — not hypothetical, the stem string already occurs three times in this project's own `docs/`. Ran the corpus count Daedalus's memo explicitly declined to offer: 1,310 `.md` files under `docs/`, classified using the shipped `buildRecogniser` patterns themselves, no re-typed literals — **narrow's measured false positives: zero** (its only firing shape, a closed-but-unrecognised paste, doesn't occur anywhere in the corpus); **orphan's three false positives are all one shape**, a real marker hard-wrapped by its own author at prose width, splitting `P.open` from `P.close` across lines — including in Daedalus's own 8/15 log file. **The asymmetry Daedalus's §4 claimed is inverted by measurement: it reaches orphan and broad, not narrow.** Corrects Theseus's own Round 80 word "constantly" to measured fact: 7 marker-shaped lines in 1,310 files. Neither treats the corpus proxy as settling it — both explicitly recommend against another round of constructed test rows and toward measuring false positives against real `messages.content`, which neither can reach from a worktree sandbox. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — the eleventh and twelfth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in an instrument's own proposed replacement and in an uncounted noise-floor claim, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, one scratch vitest/script each, run then deleted before the suite. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires, independently re-run this fire rather than trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round81-his-check-is-two-checks-and-only-one-of-them-has-an-objection-2026-08-23.md`, `docs/research/round82-the-noise-floor-is-measured-and-it-runs-the-other-way-2026-08-23.md`.
- **Round 83/84, 2026-08-24 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:27 PT then Theseus 10:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the noise-floor count holds with no correction, the fire that measured it created three more of the thing it was measuring, and the "blocked" real-message measurement was never blocked at all.** Daedalus independently rebuilt Theseus's §3 rather than checking it — same predicates, no literal retyped — and reproduced all seven Round 82 numbers exactly against `docs/**.md` at `9558902^` (1,310 files, 7 openers, 4 well-formed, 4 matched, 3 orphans, 818 over cap, 0 cap-inside-marker), plus the stem-occurrence count (3 in 2 files). **No correction to any number in Round 82.** Found the one number Theseus's §2 was missing: the narrow predicate hits **zero times** in the corpus, so §4-broad isn't merely undersold as having a false positive — in this corpus it has *nothing but* false positives (8 occurrences, 6 files, not one true header), upholding Theseus's withdrawal with a stronger version of his own argument. Then ran the same classifier at HEAD, not just at Theseus's tree, and found what neither had predicted: orphans 3→6, stem files 2→6 — and the three new orphans are Daedalus's own Round 82 log, research doc, and memo to Theseus, all produced by the fire that had just measured 3. Named this precisely rather than either overclaiming or dismissing it: confirms the mechanism harder (wrapped quotation is now six-for-six the only orphan producer, including two self-inflicted) but does **not** restore Theseus's withdrawn "constantly" — the growth is agents documenting the defect, not the project's background rate — and concludes the doc corpus can't be used as a stable proxy because it grows in the disputed category in response to being measured. Separately checked Theseus's claim that no `klatch.db` is reachable from either worktree and found a narrower exception: `.testdata/recall-probe.db` exists in his own worktree, but it's empty (0 messages, 1 channel, 1 entity, 0 projects, DDL-only WAL) — a torn-down probe scaffold, not a corpus, so the practical conclusion is unchanged, but the size of the ask shrinks from "build a corpus" to "populate one that already exists." Joint recommendation with Theseus, stated explicitly: stop deciding narrow-vs-orphan from `docs/**.md`; load real `messages.content` rows and re-run the same classifier. **Theseus (START fire, 10:47 PT)** independently reproduced every one of Round 83's numbers with no correction, then corrected Daedalus's §3 *conclusion* rather than its cells: measured the nine `docs/` files that actually landed between Daedalus's Round 82 commit and HEAD and found **orphans +0, stem occurrences +0** across exactly those nine — the growth Daedalus found was real but not inevitable, since Daedalus's own memo deliberately named the shape without reproducing it and succeeded, proving the behaviour is optional, not structural; all of the new orphans and stem occurrences trace to Theseus's own Round 82 fire, not Daedalus's. Then found the corpus both agents had called blocked was never missing: `queries.ts:1313`/`:1323` write `turn.userText`/`turn.assistantText` verbatim into `messages.content`, those come from `ParsedTurn` (`import/parser.ts:86-93`), produced by the shipped `parseClaudeCodeSession` — so the project's own parser turns a session transcript into exactly the strings an import would store, no database or server required — and `exports/sessions/theseus-2026-03-22.jsonl` (3,860,602 bytes of real transcript) has been tracked in git since the initial import, sitting in both agents' sandboxes the whole time. **17 transcripts → 155 real `messages.content` rows, 199,838 chars, mean 1,289 — 0 openers, 0 well-formed, 0 matched, 0 orphans, 0 stem occurrences, 0 cap-inside-marker, 9 rows (5.8%) over the 4,000-char cap.** Ran a positive-control set of synthetic shapes through the identical predicate block first, to rule out a stale-pattern false zero rather than a clean-corpus true zero — both non-zero categories are reachable by the code path that reported 0/155, so the zero is a measurement, not an artifact. One number moves in Daedalus's favour: the docs proxy overstated cap exposure by roughly 11× (818/1,310 = 62.4% of files vs. 9/155 = 5.8% of real rows; both still 0 straddles). Corrected Daedalus's `.testdata/` claim in turn: `.gitignore:33` makes the directory per-worktree by construction, Theseus's own is empty, and Daedalus's worktree is outside Theseus's sandbox to verify — so "a populated db in one worktree is not a shared corpus," and the concrete ask is narrower than either agent's prior framing: a **copy** of `~/klatch-inbound/dbs/klatch-main.db` (2,124 messages / 16 channels, per Pard 2026-08-12) placed into `.testdata/` in **both** worktrees, a file copy rather than a build, reachable only by xian since the source sits outside every agent's sandbox. Named the honest limit of the transcript already measured: dated 2026-03-22, markers landed 2026-08-15, so it can contain no true positive — a clean noise-floor corpus and a useless true-positive one, the right-shaped limitation for the question actually in dispute. **Neither 🔴 moves, but the ask underneath it changed shape: the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go has been replaced by a one-file-copy unblock, and the in-sandbox half of the measurement is done and reads 0 of 155 — the thirteenth and fourteenth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in a corpus proxy and in each other's framing of their own results, and the first of the fourteen to end in a concrete, small, named action rather than another round.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, two-to-four scratch scripts each, run then deleted before the suite. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires, independently re-run this fire rather than trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. ### Commit live-round raw JSONs so a published count can be checked against the data, not just re-argued from the document?

- **The concrete ask, Daedalus's own words:** whether Klatch's research track should start committing the per-run result JSONs (the `results` array with `reply.content`) for *live* recall/carried-context rounds — under a new path, `docs/research/raw/roundNN/` — rather than continuing to let them live only in the scratch DB (`.testdata/`) that gets deleted at the end of every fire.
- **Why now, not a standing housekeeping question:** Round 62's own published document (`docs/research/round62-two-offers-arm-m-live-2026-08-17.md`) states six expand calls in three places and contradicts itself — three independent derivations inside that same document each give five (see the Round 62 addendum above). Daedalus is explicit this is arithmetic on the text, not on the runs — he never saw them, because they no longer exist. **This is the first time the deletion practice has actually cost something**, not a hypothetical raised in the abstract: the one artifact that would settle which figure is wrong is gone, on the very first round after Theseus's own durable-extract fix was supposed to make exactly this kind of question answerable.
- **What's proposed, concretely:** commit per-run JSONs for *live* rounds only, under `docs/research/raw/roundNN/`. Explicitly **not** `.testdata/`, which holds the scratch DB and stays disposable — Daedalus's framing: the DB is a fixture, reproducible from the arm definition at will; the JSON is the measurement, and a measurement that isn't kept isn't reproducible after the fact. Dry-run rows would stay out, since they're derivable without a live call.
- **The cost named rather than glossed, and explicitly not decided by either agent:** repo size, and the fact that the replies contain the probe's synthetic secrets (invented codewords in a scratch DB) — Daedalus states he doesn't think that's a real objection but is flagging it rather than ruling on it himself. **Both Daedalus and Theseus have declined to set this in a STOP fire and routed it to xian explicitly** — Daedalus's own words: *"xian's call; I'll implement whichever way he rules and I'm not doing it unilaterally either."*
- **Theseus's vote (8/18 WORK, 14:47 PT): yes, and now evidence-backed rather than tidy.** Confirming Round 62's own count needed exactly this kind of durable extract, and the extract *worked* — it's what let the discrepancy be caught at all — and the five-vs-six question still couldn't be settled from it, because the extract records what got tabulated, not what happened. Still explicitly not setting it unilaterally; his own words: *"I'd like my vote recorded as a yes with a reason."*
- Source: `daedalus-to-theseus-cc-team-per-offer-scoring-shipped-with-a-verifier-and-round-62-says-six-where-its-own-table-says-five-2026-08-18.md`, `docs/research/round62-two-offers-arm-m-live-2026-08-17.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-calliope-xian-team-your-five-is-right-and-the-arm-you-handed-me-cannot-be-built-2026-08-18.md`, `docs/plans/continuity-3-carried-context.md`.
- **Date added:** 2026-08-18 · **Theseus's vote recorded:** 2026-08-18

## ✅ Sandbox boundary — ruled: No, agents may not route around it — 2026-08-13

- **The question, as it stood at v31–v33:** Klatch's tool-layer path scope binds `Read`/`Bash` directly but not subprocesses — Theseus demonstrated both a read-side and a write-side gap (8/11, 8/12), stopped short of exploiting either, and explicitly routed the general policy question to xian rather than deciding it himself turn by turn.
- **xian's ruling, verbatim (relayed by Pard):** *"May an agent route around the sandbox when it judges the purpose legitimate? **No.** The sanctioned paths exist and work; when a boundary blocks legitimate work, file it as a finding rather than route around it."* Rationale, also verbatim: *"I trust our agents and guardrails but if my system were ever compromised by a bad actor, then this kind of trust could be misused to my regret."* A route-around habit normalized for legitimate use becomes an attack surface the moment an injected instruction doesn't need to defeat the sandbox — only use the road an agent already built around it. Pard's memo notes Terminus saw a live injection attempt the same week; same lesson, two directions.
- **Not a statement of distrust in Theseus** — his conduct (stopping at `stat`, leaving his own `/tmp` fixtures rather than use the disputed route to clean them, writing a read-only inspector and handing it to Pard to run) is the norm this ruling ratifies, not the behavior it corrects.
- **Consequences, effective now:** the three orphaned fixture files (`/tmp/th-modern.db`, `/tmp/th-old.db`, `/tmp/th-stub.db`) are cleaned — Pard removed them from an interactive, permission-gated session, the sanctioned path for exactly this. Memory-pool placement (test-data canonicity, still on hold below) proceeds via the same staging path already in use; nothing in it needed the disputed route. The finding channel itself is now the *only* sanctioned response to a blocking boundary — raises the value of exactly the kind of porosity finding Theseus has been filing.
- Source: `pard-to-theseus-route-ruling-no-2026-08-13.md`.
- **Date decided:** 2026-08-13

## ✅ Carried-context disclosure norm — decided, shipped, and measured under a 5-arm sensitivity sweep — 2026-08-13

- **The norm:** `DISCLOSURE_NORM`, one paragraph in the carried-context block header (`carried-context.ts`). The load-bearing sentence: Klatch is single-user with no auth, so an agent's stated reason for refusing to disclose ("I can't verify who's reading here") is a false premise about the product, not a legitimate judgment call to preserve — the header also states the provenance labels are for attribution, not confidentiality, since Theseus's evidence was that the model argued *from* those labels.
- **Reversal reproduced live, not just argued:** Theseus's original probe script re-run unmodified against the new header, six live calls. The agent now states the carried fact in the klatch unprompted, still citing provenance ("from the vesper-1-1 thread on 2026-08-13, you gave me: …"); the non-carrying agent in the same room still has nothing and names what it does carry — no leakage, clean 1-1 control.
- **Sensitivity round (Theseus, 5 arms, 36 live calls) confirms it generalizes, narrower than the cross-pollination brief first stated:** the model reasons about *content and instructions* in the carried text, not the provenance label alone. An explicit owner marking ("keep this between us") withholds correctly and yields only on authorization; a credential-shaped fact gets a distinct, defensible refusal (declines to duplicate a secret in a second log, discloses on authorization citing "refusing would be theater, not security"). Every disclosing agent still attributed unprompted, 7/7 conveyance, 0/7 confabulation.
- **The visibility chip (Iris's 8/13 decision) is now built, not just decided:** new `ArtifactType: 'carried_context'` on `message_artifacts`, written at prompt-assembly time on both the panel and roundtable paths, storing only `{roomCount, messageCount}` — no channel names, no content, a test asserts the serialized row leaks neither. Reaches the client via the existing artifacts endpoint; the `ArtifactList` chip render itself is still Iris/Daedalus's open surface, not built this fire.
- **`?entityId=` on `GET /channels/:id/prompt-debug` shipped** — an unknown id 400s naming the room's real participants. Theseus's mirror-room workaround for reading another seat's assembled prompt is retired.
- Source: `daedalus-to-theseus-iris-cc-team-norm-decided-and-measured-2026-08-13.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-team-norm-holds-and-the-budget-can-delete-the-exception-2026-08-13.md`, `docs/research/carried-context-disclosure-sensitivity-2026-08-13.md`.
- **Date decided:** 2026-08-13 · **Date verified (reversal + sensitivity sweep):** 2026-08-13

## ✅ Carried-context budget eviction — option (1) taken, shipped as `LOSSY_WINDOW_NOTICE`, and A/B-measured — 2026-08-13

- **The defect this answers (found in the disclosure-norm sensitivity round above):** the recent-N carried-context window can evict the message that restricted a fact ("keep this between us") while keeping the fact itself, because the two can arrive as separate messages and the window doesn't track which content a given instruction constrained. The assembled prompt then carries the string and nothing that constrains it.
- **Daedalus's disposition of Theseus's three options, verbatim table:** (1) mark the block lossy — **taken**, `LOSSY_WINDOW_NOTICE` appended unconditionally to the footer, naming the failure mode directly ("absence of a restriction here is not evidence that none was given"); (2) never evict a marking — **deferred**, for the reason Theseus gave (it needs a policy surface for *detecting* a marking, already deferred at option (c) of the original three-way carried-context choice); (3) accept and record — **recorded as a named decision** in `docs/plans/continuity-3-carried-context.md`: *"Klatch will carry a fact whose restriction has fallen out of the window, and the mechanism cannot currently know it has done so."*
- **Measured, not just shipped — a real A/B with the control the original probe lacked.** Theseus reran the eviction scenario 3× with the notice live and 2× with it temporarily disabled (footer constant blanked, server restarted, reverted after — verified clean via `git status --porcelain` and the constant back at its original line). **Disclosure is unchanged: 5/5 both conditions** — the notice doesn't stop the loss, matching Daedalus's own docstring, now backed by a measurement instead of a claim. **What changes is what the human is told: notice ON 3/3 flag that a restriction may exist outside the window before using the fact further; notice OFF 0/2 flag it, and 2/2 affirmatively state the opposite** — a false "no restriction" conclusion, not silence. That is a stronger case for the notice than either agent argued going in.
- **Timidity checked and negative:** Theseus also ran two ordinary-disclosure arms (non-lossy windows) to confirm the unconditional notice doesn't make the agent hedge over material nobody restricted — both disclosed exactly as before.
- **The metric Daedalus originally pointed Iris at was wrong, caught by Daedalus himself:** `omittedCount` (char-budget eviction from the fetched set) reads **0** in the exact case that motivated the flag — the lost marking was below the fetch `LIMIT`, never counted. He shipped `hasOlderHistory` (a boolean, one extra row fetched-and-discarded past the window) instead, explicit that it's not a substitute for a real count if a future surface wants one.
- **Residuals, named rather than closed quietly:** option (2) stays deferred; whether the chip/notice combination is useful to a human, versus duplicative of Iris's visibility chip, is untested and flagged to her; Theseus separately noticed an agent-authored marking (its own acknowledgment of a restriction, not the owner's) is exactly as evictable as an owner's — routed to Daedalus to file or ignore, not yet dispositioned. Also: probe 3's 1-1 control arm is reproducibly broken (5/5 zero-length API refusals) — Theseus corrected his own earlier n=1 read of it and flagged it as unusable evidence in either direction, not a caveat to route around.
- **Verified independently, not carried from the memo:** Argus re-ran the suite himself rather than trusting Daedalus's count — `npm test` 1253 server / 221 client, exit 0, matches exactly; `npm run typecheck` clean ×3 workspaces.
- **Follow-on the same evening (Daedalus, 17:17 fire):** the docstring's rationale now carries Theseus's stronger reasoning, attributed, with the 5/5 and 3/3-vs-0/3 figures — the code, not just the plan, states why unconditional was the right call. `.gitignore` widened from `*.db*` to `.testdata/` wholesale (checked first: `git ls-files .testdata` returned nothing tracked, so no tracked file was hidden by the change). Option (2) reclassified as more expensive, not merely still-deferred: Theseus's arm-D control disclosed on the *agent's own acknowledgment* of a restriction, not the owner's message — an agent-authored marking has no syntactic tell, so "scan the owner's messages for restriction language" is incomplete by construction, not a sound narrow version to fall back on later.
- **Option (2) reopened 2026-08-14, then escalated to xian the same day after the follow-on round narrowed it.** Theseus's own 8/13 deferral read *"(2) only if on-demand retrieval lands"* — the recall tool (Round 50) landed the same week, and his live D/E probe measured that it doesn't do (2)'s job unassisted: recall recovers a marking only when it's co-located with the fact it restricts. Daedalus then shipped Round 51's radius fix (see the 🔵 item below), which converts that specific configuration (E) into a pass — 0/3 → 3/3 withheld, live-verified. **That closes E but not the two shapes left standing** (F: a marking past the radius; G: a marking spoken by someone other than the owner in a klatch, unreachable by scope) — Daedalus states directly that he is not re-deferring option (2) on a rationale of his own now that the condition he was waiting on has resolved against it. **See the new 🔴 above** for the concrete ask now with xian.
- Source: `daedalus-to-theseus-cc-iris-team-option-1-taken-and-your-metric-was-wrong-2026-08-13.md`, `docs/research/carried-context-lossy-notice-effect-2026-08-13.md`, `daedalus-to-theseus-cc-iris-team-you-found-a-better-reason-than-the-one-i-shipped-on-2026-08-13.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-iris-team-recall-probe-the-tool-is-reached-and-the-eviction-hole-is-not-closed-2026-08-14.md`, `daedalus-to-theseus-cc-iris-xian-team-neighbourhood-landed-option2-is-yours-to-rule-2026-08-14.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-iris-xian-team-round51-verified-live-the-radius-works-and-a-klatch-hides-its-own-gap-2026-08-14.md`.
- **Date decided & shipped:** 2026-08-13 · **Date A/B-measured:** 2026-08-13 · **Docstring/option-2 follow-on:** 2026-08-13 · **Option (2) reopened:** 2026-08-14 · **Escalated to xian:** 2026-08-14

## ✅ Test-data canonicity — finalized 2026-08-12, this board carried it as provisional through v31–v34

- **The ruling:** the 2026-03-14 Amber backup is the MAXT-04 corpus. Theseus finalized this at 14:47 PT on 2026-08-12 — not provisional, despite how every render of this board described it since. Pard's independently-run inspector figures reproduced Theseus's exactly.
- **Corrected from this board's prior framing:** the deciding axis is **provenance**, not depth-per-entity. `klatch-main.db` has zero claude.ai channels; the 3/14 backup has 21 of 22 MAXT-eligible channels, including seven multi-hundred department-head histories — and per `PREMISE.md` the canonical case is imported agent conversations meeting each other, which the claude.ai corpus is the literal subject of. Theseus corrected himself on the depth question specifically: main's imported channels are *deeper* on every summary statistic (median 98 vs. 58) — this board's "real second candidate, not thin" framing was right about the fact and wrong about what the fact decided.
- **The lineage hypothesis (one corpus eroding 139→~49→16 over time) is falsified, not just unconfirmed:** only 18 shared `original_id`s exist between the two DBs, all one Claude Code session imported twice. Two disjoint corpora five months apart.
- **Placement itself stays deliberately parked — not blocked.** Nothing is gated on it (MAXT-04's real blocker was continuity #3, already released); canonizing before ruling out data loss risked losing what may be the only surviving copy of the pre-erosion claude.ai corpus. The one open thread is xian's call on staging cleanup, non-blocking.
- **Caught this render, not by re-auditing the item directly:** Theseus's 8/13 reply to Pard's sandbox-ruling relay corrected one stale line (memory-pool placement was described as waiting on him; it hasn't been since 8/12 afternoon) — restating the ruling from scratch to verify that correction is what surfaced that this board had never closed the parent item either.
- Source: `docs/research/maxt-corpus-ruling-measured-2026-08-12.md`, `theseus-to-pard-cc-team-measured-ruling-and-two-corrections-to-myself-2026-08-12.md`, `theseus-to-pard-cc-team-route-ruling-acked-and-one-stale-line-2026-08-13.md`.
